10 月非农：就业市场短期修复，但结构性分化与
长期失业压力仍存

宏观经济点评
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2
近期有所走强，主要集中在会计师事务所和咨询机构。根据褐皮书的调
查，高利率环境叠加关税成本上升，推动破产相关法律服务需求增加。不
过，机构对后续业务增长仍持相对谨慎的态度，预计整体业务仅温和增
长。经济和政治不确定性，以及燃油、商品成本上升，或促使部分客户收
缩支出。此外，本月较高的就业增量可能受到选举相关活动的短期推动。
波士顿学院经济学家布莱恩·贝休恩认为，上月专业和商业服务就业激增
可能存在一定的选举因素：各竞选团队增加了对咨询、广告及临时用工的
投入，随着相关活动结束，部分岗位需求可能随之消退，预计 11 月相关
机构或裁员约 2 万人。因此，若这一判断成立，10 月专业和商业服务的
高增长可能在后续月份出现部分回吐，其持续性仍有待观察。
二是医疗和社会救助行业的就业有所回升，政策变化可能带来中期上行空
间。医疗和社会救助新增 5.5 万人，较此前有所改善，呈现一定的触底回
升迹象。其中，医疗保健服务增加 4.2 万人，高于过去 12 个月 3.1 万人

的月均水平；社会援助增加 1.4 万人。相较于 10 月的就业增量，后续政
策变化可能是影响该行业中期就业走势的另一项重要变量。中期选举后，
民主党取得参众两院控制权，医疗可负担性议题可能获得更高的立法优先
级。选前调查显示，医疗负担能力是选民关注的重要议题之一，相关选民
诉求可能推动民主党加强对《平价医疗法案》（ACA）和医疗补助计划
（Medicaid）相关政策的监督，并尝试通过立法缓解保费和药价负担。若
相关政策调整得以推进，医疗服务需求及相关就业可能获得额外支撑，医
疗行业就业的中期增长中枢或有所上移。
三是假日季前补员或带动下游就业，但上游批发却有所走弱。运输和仓
储、零售与批发均属于贸易、运输和公用事业大类。10 月这几个行业的
就业变化，可能在一定程度上反映了假日购物季前的季节性招聘，但上下
游表现并不一致。运输和仓储增加 2.5 万人，为 4 月以来最大单月增幅，
其中快递及邮政投递增加 1.7 万人。Challenger 数据显示，亚马逊 10 月
公布的 30.0 万个招聘计划几乎全部属于季节性岗位，主要集中在仓储和

电商物流领域。因此，本月运输仓储就业增长可能更多反映企业为假日购
物季提前补充人手，而非物流需求出现趋势性扩张。零售业增加 0.6 万
人，连续第八个月实现增长。分项来看，综合商品零售商和服装、服饰配
件、鞋类及珠宝零售商是主要支撑。其中，百货商店增加 0.7 万人，仓储
式会员店、大型综合超市及其他综合商品零售商合计增加 1.2 万人，服
装、服饰配件、鞋类及珠宝零售商增加 1.3 万人。不过，零售就业增长同
样带有较强的季节性特征。Challenger 指出，部分零售商在裁减长期岗位
的同时，为假日购物季增加临时招聘，反映企业可能更倾向于通过季节性
用工应对短期需求，而非扩大长期雇佣规模。与运输仓储和零售业的补员
形成对比的是，批发业在连续六个月增长后，于 10 月减少 0.9 万人。其
中，耐用品批发商减少 0.3 万人，非耐用品批发商减少 0.8 万人。
四是其他行业表现仍存结构性分化：信息、金融业边际改善，休闲酒店业
有所走弱。信息业减少 0.1 万人，但近三个月就业降幅有所收窄，行业内

### Page 3

宏观经济点评
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3
此前负增长转为正增长。其中，金融及保险业增加 0.1 万人，房地产、租
赁及出租业增加 0.6 万人。本月信息业就业降幅收窄、金融业就业转正，
可能反映部分细分领域招聘需求有所改善。不过，信息业和金融业均受到
AI 技术发展的影响较多，目前单月数据尚不足以确认行业就业趋势已经反
转，后续仍需观察改善能否持续。休闲及酒店业减少 1.3 万人，主要受到
餐饮服务就业回落的影响。此前两个月，餐饮服务就业累计增加 4.3 万
人。但是餐饮服务业短期波动相对明显，因此本月就业下降可能包含前期
招聘增长后的季节性回吐，暂时还不足以据此判断居民服务消费需求明显
走弱。
商品业中主要是制造业支撑就业增长，新增岗位集中于 AI 相关耐用品。
商品部门新增就业约 1.5 万人，增量几乎全部来自制造业，增加 1.9 万
人，连续第五个月实现增长，而非耐用品制造业减少 0.6 万人。从需求端
看，制造业景气度也有所改善。10 月 ISM 制造业 PMI 升至 55.5，高于 9

月的 54.5 及市场预期的 54.6，创近四年来第二高的扩张水平。其中，生
产指数由 56.7 升至 59.0，新订单指数由 55.3 小幅升至 55.5。生产与新订
单指数同步处于扩张区间，表明制造业供需两端均有所改善，短期内或继
续为制造业就业提供支撑。从行业结构来看，计算机及电子产品、金属制
品和机械设备三个行业新增就业较多，合计增加 1.6 万人，或与人工智
能、数据中心、半导体及相关基础设施领域的需求增长有关。
政府就业里主要是州政府提供支撑，联邦与地方政府就业小幅收缩。政府
部门新增就业 0.6 万人，仅占全部新增非农就业的约 4%，对本月整体就
业增长的贡献相对有限。从内部结构来看，政府就业呈现分化：州政府增
加 0.8 万人，联邦政府和地方政府则分别减少 0.1 万人。
劳动力市场供需两端边际改善，但就业结构仍存分化，长期失业压力上
升。
美国劳动力需求出现边际改善，企业裁员意愿偏低，招聘活动有所回暖。
Challenger 数据显示，10 月美国雇主宣布裁员 43,994 人，略高于 9 月的

43,281 人，但较 2025 年 10 月的 153,074 人大幅下降 71%，为 2023 年
以来同月最低水平。从招聘需求来看，Indeed 招聘广告数量自 6 月触底
后缓慢回升，但扩张力度仍然有限。此外，企业也可能通过调整员工工时
而非直接裁员来应对需求变化。10 月私人非农平均每周工时维持 34.4 小
时，为 2024 年 3 月以来最高水平，企业可能通过提高现有员工的劳动投
入来满足生产需求；不过制造业平均每周工时为 40.5 小时，加班时间增
加 0.1 小时至 3.1 小时，显示需求与资本品订单的边际拉动有限。
潜在劳动力供给有所累积，就业意愿与求职信心出现背离。10 月劳动参
与率由 61.8%回升至 61.9%，劳动力人口增加 42.1 万人，表明劳动力供
给有所扩张。其中，25—54 岁核心劳动年龄人口劳动参与率为 83.7%。
与此同时，非劳动力人口内部仍存在一定的潜在供给。非劳动力人口中希
望就业但未积极求职人口的人数占比由 53.5%升至 54.5%，显示有意愿就

### Page 4

宏观经济点评
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 4
升至 0.43%，反映部分潜在求职者可能因就业机会不足或求职受挫而暂时
退出劳动力市场。
从就业结构来看，总量改善之下，质量仍存在分化。10 月就业人口增加
46.3 万人，高于劳动力人口增幅，意味着当月就业增长不仅吸收了新增劳
动力供给，也消化了部分存量失业人口，推动失业率由 4.2%降至 4.1%。
劳动参与率同步回升，说明失业率下降并非单纯由劳动者退出劳动力市场
所推动，就业市场总量表现有所改善。全职就业人数自 7 月近期低点以来
波动上行，显示全职岗位有所修复，但恢复过程仍不稳定。部分劳动者需
要通过同时从事多份工作来增加收入，多重职业者占总就业人口的比例升
至 5.4%，仍处于 2024 年以来 5.1%—5.5%的区间上沿。与此同时，因经
济原因从事兼职的人数环比增加 25.7 万人，反映部分劳动者仍面临全职
岗位不足或工作时长受限的问题。
从失业结构与失业时长来看，主动离职意愿偏弱，长期失业压力上升。主
动离职者占失业人口的比例由 10.5%升至 10.9%，但仍低于过去 12 个月

11.8%的均值，说明劳动者主动辞职、寻找更好工作机会的意愿尚未明显
恢复。重新进入劳动力市场者人数小幅升至 3.28 万人，高于过去 12 个月
均值，反映部分此前退出劳动力市场的人群正在重新求职。与此同时，长
期失业压力仍在累积，部分失业者重新就业的难度可能有所增加，劳动力
市场内部的就业再配置压力尚未明显缓解。10 月失业 27 周以上的人数增
加 1.9 万人至 196.3 万人，占失业人口的比例升至 27.6%，为 2021 年底
以来最高水平；失业中位时长升至 11.9 周，平均失业时长达到 27 周。
工资增长呈现环比反弹、同比放缓的分化特征。10 月私人非农平均时薪
环比上涨 0.37%，为 2025 年 11 月以来最大单月涨幅，后续仍需关注工
资环比增速能否持续维持高位；同比上涨 3.12%，仍处于缓慢回落趋势。
分行业来看，建筑业时薪环比上涨 0.91%，涨幅相对突出；制造业上涨
0.49%；专业和商业服务、教育和健康服务均上涨 0.33%。
就业短期修复提振市场，但结构性分化与持续性仍待观察。

10 月就业数据公布后，美股内部结构分化，利率重新定价与 AI 产业逻辑
交织。纳斯达克综合指数上涨 1.09%，道琼斯工业平均指数下跌 0.46%，
标普 500 指数上涨 0.31%，科技成长股明显跑赢，三大指数表现分化。债
市方面，2 年期和 10 年期美债收益率分别上行 4.9bp 和 2.4bp，收益率曲
线熊平，或反映市场对美联储降息路径重新定价，短端利率上行压力更
大。与此同时，美元指数上涨 0.68%，COMEX 黄金下跌 0.83%。整体来
看，10 月美国劳动力市场供需两端均有所改善，新增就业明显回升，失
业率下降，劳动参与率同步上升，显示就业市场短期修复力度有所增强。
但就业增长仍集中于少数行业，长期失业压力上升、非自愿兼职增加，表
明就业市场尚未实现全面、均衡的改善。往后看，就业市场回暖的持续性
仍需进一步观察，一方面，随着假日季临时招聘及选举相关用工需求逐步
消退，需关注就业增量能否保持韧性，以及招聘需求能否向更多行业扩
散；另一方面，需关注工资环比增速反弹能否延续，以及长期失业、非自

1. 就业市场短期修复，但结构性分化与长期失业压力仍存 
1.1 10 月美国就业市场明显走强，劳动力供需同步改善 
10 月美国非农就业新增 16.5 万人，大幅高于市场预期的 6.5 万人，也高于过去 12
个月 6.1 万人的月均增幅。与此同时，8 月和 9 月新增就业人数分别由 13.3 万人和 2.9
万人上修至 18.6 万人和 6.0 万人，合计上修 8.4 万人，推动近三个月新增就业均值升至
13.7 万人，为 2025 年 2 月以来最高，显示就业市场修复力度有所增强。家庭调查数据
同样表现强劲。10 月就业人口增加 46.3 万人，高于上月的 40.6 万人；U3 失业率由 4.2%
降至 4.1%，劳动参与率由 61.8%升至 61.9%。延续 7 月以来的回升趋势。劳动力参与
率回升的同时失业率有所下降，或反映劳动力市场需求有所改善。就业数据公布后，市
场对美联储政策路径的预期偏鹰：12 月加息一次的概率升至 78.8%；对于 2027 年 6 月
的政策路径，市场定价在此前加息基础上再加息一次的概率为 38.9%，维持利率不变的
概率为 33.9%，两者差距较小，表明市场虽倾向于进一步加息，但对后续政策路径仍存
在较大分歧。 
1.2 行业扩散有所改善，但新增就业仍集中于少数领域，结构性分化明显 
10 月非农新增就业 16.5 万人，其中私人部门增加 15.9 万人，政府部门增加 0.6 万
人，新增就业几乎全部来自私人部门。而私人部门新增就业主要由服务部门贡献，增加
14.4 万人。从就业扩散情况看，行业扩散指数由 9 月修正后的 54.2 升至 57.6，三个月
均值也升至今年以来最高水平，表明新增就业涉及的行业范围有所扩大。不过，从具体
行业贡献来看，就业增长仍存在明显的结构性分化：专业和商业服务、医疗和社会救助、
运输和仓储三大行业合计新增约 15 万人，占全部新增非农就业的九成以上，表明本轮
就业增长仍主要由少数行业支撑，尚未形成广泛、均衡的招聘回暖。 
服务业方面主要呈现四大特征： 
一是专业和商业服务贡献最大，但增量可能包含一次性因素。专业和商业服务新增
7.0 万人，占全部新增非农就业的 42.4%，为 2022 年 7 月以来最大单月增幅，明显高于
过去 12 个月 1.5 万人的月均水平。分项来看，专业、科学和技术服务增加 3.3 万人，其
中管理、科学和技术咨询服务贡献 1.7 万人；行政和支持服务增加 2.8 万人，其中临时
帮助服务增加 1.9 万人；企业管理增加 0.9 万人。从需求端反馈来看，专业和商业服务
需求近期有所走强，主要集中在会计师事务所和咨询机构。根据褐皮书的调查，高利率
环境叠加关税成本上升，推动破产相关法律服务需求增加。不过，机构对后续业务增长
仍持相对谨慎的态度，预计整体业务仅温和增长。经济和政治不确定性，以及燃油、商
品成本上升，或促使部分客户收缩支出1。此外，本月较高的就业增量可能受到选举相关
活动的短期推动。波士顿学院经济学家布莱恩·贝休恩认为，上月专业和商业服务就业激
增可能存在一定的选举因素：各竞选团队增加了对咨询、广告及临时用工的投入，随着
相关活动结束，部分岗位需求可能随之消退，预计 11 月相关机构或裁员约 2 万人2。因
此，若这一判断成立，10 月专业和商业服务的高增长可能在后续月份出现部分回吐，其
持续性仍有待观察。 
二是医疗和社会救助行业的就业有所回升，政策变化可能带来中期上行空间。医疗
和社会救助新增 5.5 万人，较此前有所改善，呈现一定的触底回升迹象。其中，医疗保
健服务增加 4.2 万人，高于过去 12 个月 3.1 万人的月均水平；社会援助增加 1.4 万人。
相较于 10 月的就业增量，后续政策变化可能是影响该行业中期就业走势的另一项重要
变量。中期选举后，民主党取得参众两院控制权，医疗可负担性议题可能获得更高的立
法优先级。选前调查显示，医疗负担能力是选民关注的重要议题之一，相关选民诉求可
能推动民主党加强对《平价医疗法案》（ACA）和医疗补助计划（Medicaid）相关政策的

### Page 9

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 9 
监督，并尝试通过立法缓解保费和药价负担3。若相关政策调整得以推进，医疗服务需求
及相关就业可能获得额外支撑，医疗行业就业的中期增长中枢或有所上移。 
三是假日季前补员或带动下游就业，但上游批发却有所走弱。运输和仓储、零售与
批发均属于贸易、运输和公用事业大类。10 月这几个行业的就业变化，可能在一定程度
上反映了假日购物季前的季节性招聘，但上下游表现并不一致。运输和仓储增加 2.5 万
人，为 4 月以来最大单月增幅，其中快递及邮政投递增加 1.7 万人。Challenger 数据显
示，亚马逊 10 月公布的 30.0 万个招聘计划几乎全部属于季节性岗位，主要集中在仓储
和电商物流领域4。因此，本月运输仓储就业增长可能更多反映企业为假日购物季提前补
充人手，而非物流需求出现趋势性扩张。零售业增加 0.6 万人，连续第八个月实现增长。
分项来看，综合商品零售商和服装、服饰配件、鞋类及珠宝零售商是主要支撑。其中，
百货商店增加 0.7 万人，仓储式会员店、大型综合超市及其他综合商品零售商合计增加
1.2 万人，服装、服饰配件、鞋类及珠宝零售商增加 1.3 万人。不过，零售就业增长同样
带有较强的季节性特征。Challenger 指出，部分零售商在裁减长期岗位的同时，为假日
购物季增加临时招聘，反映企业可能更倾向于通过季节性用工应对短期需求，而非扩大
长期雇佣规模。与运输仓储和零售业的补员形成对比的是，批发业在连续六个月增长后，
于 10 月减少 0.9 万人。其中，耐用品批发商减少 0.3 万人，非耐用品批发商减少 0.8 万
人。 
四是其他行业表现仍存结构性分化：信息、金融业边际改善，休闲酒店业有所走弱。
信息业减少 0.1 万人，但近三个月就业降幅有所收窄，行业内部也出现一定分化。其中，
计算基础设施、数据处理、网络托管及相关服务就业持平，电影及录音产业增加 0.3 万
人。金融业则增加 0.7 万人，由此前负增长转为正增长。其中，金融及保险业增加 0.1
万人，房地产、租赁及出租业增加 0.6 万人。本月信息业就业降幅收窄、金融业就业转
正，可能反映部分细分领域招聘需求有所改善。不过，信息业和金融业均受到 AI 技术发
展的影响较多，目前单月数据尚不足以确认行业就业趋势已经反转，后续仍需观察改善
能否持续。休闲及酒店业减少 1.3 万人，主要受到餐饮服务就业回落的影响。此前两个
月，餐饮服务就业累计增加 4.3 万人。但是餐饮服务业短期波动相对明显，因此本月就
业下降可能包含前期招聘增长后的季节性回吐，暂时还不足以据此判断居民服务消费需
求明显走弱。 
商品业中主要是制造业支撑就业增长，新增岗位集中于 AI 相关耐用品。商品部门
新增就业约 1.5 万人，增量几乎全部来自制造业，增加 1.9 万人，连续第五个月实现增
长，而非耐用品制造业减少 0.6 万人。从需求端看，制造业景气度也有所改善。10 月 ISM
制造业 PMI 升至 55.5，高于 9 月的 54.5 及市场预期的 54.6，创近四年来第二高的扩张
水平。其中，生产指数由 56.7 升至 59.0，新订单指数由 55.3 小幅升至 55.5。生产与新
订单指数同步处于扩张区间，表明制造业供需两端均有所改善，短期内或继续为制造业
就业提供支撑。从行业结构来看，计算机及电子产品、金属制品和机械设备三个行业新
增就业较多，合计增加 1.6 万人，或与人工智能、数据中心、半导体及相关基础设施领
域的需求增长有关。 
政府就业里主要是州政府提供支撑，联邦与地方政府就业小幅收缩。政府部门新增
就业 0.6 万人，仅占全部新增非农就业的约 4%，对本月整体就业增长的贡献相对有限。
从内部结构来看，政府就业呈现分化：州政府增加 0.8 万人，联邦政府和地方政府则分
别减少 0.1 万人。 
1.3 劳动力市场供需两端边际改善，但就业结构仍存分化，长期失业压力
上升 
美国劳动力需求出现边际改善，企业裁员意愿偏低，招聘活动有所回暖。Challenger

### Page 10

宏观经济点评
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 10
数据显示，10 月美国雇主宣布裁员 43,994 人，略高于 9 月的 43,281 人，但较 2025 年
10 月的 153,074 人大幅下降 71%，为 2023 年以来同月最低水平。从招聘需求来看，
Indeed 招聘广告数量自 6 月触底后缓慢回升，但扩张力度仍然有限。此外，企业也可能
通过调整员工工时而非直接裁员来应对需求变化。10 月私人非农平均每周工时维持 34.4
小时，为 2024 年 3 月以来最高水平，企业可能通过提高现有员工的劳动投入来满足生
产需求；不过制造业平均每周工时为 40.5 小时，加班时间增加 0.1 小时至 3.1 小时，显
示需求与资本品订单的边际拉动有限。
潜在劳动力供给有所累积，就业意愿与求职信心出现背离。10 月劳动参与率由 61.8%
回升至 61.9%，劳动力人口增加 42.1 万人，表明劳动力供给有所扩张。其中，25—54
岁核心劳动年龄人口劳动参与率为 83.7%。与此同时，非劳动力人口内部仍存在一定的

潜在供给。非劳动力人口中希望就业但未积极求职人口的人数占比由 53.5%升至 54.5%，
显示有意愿就业但尚未进入劳动力市场的人群有所增加，后续若就业机会改善，部分非
劳动力人口或重新进入劳动力市场。与此同时，失望个人占比从 0.37%上升至 0.43%，
反映部分潜在求职者可能因就业机会不足或求职受挫而暂时退出劳动力市场。
从就业结构来看，总量改善之下，质量仍存在分化。10 月就业人口增加 46.3 万人，
高于劳动力人口增幅，意味着当月就业增长不仅吸收了新增劳动力供给，也消化了部分
存量失业人口，推动失业率由 4.2%降至 4.1%。劳动参与率同步回升，说明失业率下降
并非单纯由劳动者退出劳动力市场所推动，就业市场总量表现有所改善。全职就业人数
自 7 月近期低点以来波动上行，显示全职岗位有所修复，但恢复过程仍不稳定。部分劳
动者需要通过同时从事多份工作来增加收入，多重职业者占总就业人口的比例升至 5.4%，
仍处于 2024 年以来 5.1%—5.5%的区间上沿。与此同时，因经济原因从事兼职的人数

环比增加 25.7 万人，反映部分劳动者仍面临全职岗位不足或工作时长受限的问题。
从失业结构与失业时长来看，主动离职意愿偏弱，长期失业压力上升。主动离职者
占失业人口的比例由 10.5%升至 10.9%，但仍低于过去 12 个月 11.8%的均值，说明劳
动者主动辞职、寻找更好工作机会的意愿尚未明显恢复。重新进入劳动力市场者人数小
幅升至 3.28 万人，高于过去 12 个月均值，反映部分此前退出劳动力市场的人群正在重
新求职。与此同时，长期失业压力仍在累积，部分失业者重新就业的难度可能有所增加，
劳动力市场内部的就业再配置压力尚未明显缓解。10 月失业 27 周以上的人数增加 1.9
万人至 196.3 万人，占失业人口的比例升至 27.6%，为 2021 年底以来最高水平；失业
中位时长升至 11.9 周，平均失业时长达到 27 周。
工资增长呈现环比反弹、同比放缓的分化特征。10 月私人非农平均时薪环比上涨
0.37%，为 2025 年 11 月以来最大单月涨幅，后续仍需关注工资环比增速能否持续维持

高位；同比上涨 3.12%，仍处于缓慢回落趋势。分行业来看，建筑业时薪环比上涨 0.91%，
涨幅相对突出；制造业上涨 0.49%；专业和商业服务、教育和健康服务均上涨 0.33%。
1.4 就业短期修复提振市场，但结构性分化与持续性仍待观察
10 月就业数据公布后，美股内部结构分化，利率重新定价与 AI 产业逻辑交织。纳
斯达克综合指数上涨 1.09%，道琼斯工业平均指数下跌 0.46%，标普 500 指数上涨 0.31%，
科技成长股明显跑赢，三大指数表现分化。债市方面，2 年期和 10 年期美债收益率分别
上行 4.9bp 和 2.4bp，收益率曲线熊平，或反映市场对美联储降息路径重新定价，短端
利率上行压力更大。与此同时，美元指数上涨 0.68%，COMEX 黄金下跌 0.83%。整体
来看，10 月美国劳动力市场供需两端均有所改善，新增就业明显回升，失业率下降，劳
动参与率同步上升，显示就业市场短期修复力度有所增强。但就业增长仍集中于少数行
业，长期失业压力上升、非自愿兼职增加，表明就业市场尚未实现全面、均衡的改善。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价（或行业
指数）相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。其中 A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准；新三板市场以三板成指（针
对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧；其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行，通膨風險則偏向上行，因此將保留政策彈性，根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線，且創兩年半來最大漲幅，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明，強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，台股創高吸引外資匯入，推升台幣最高來到 31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至，台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪。中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市，而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局，主要在 6.7000 上方震盪，臨近周末，人行中間價為 6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來新低，不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至 4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔，市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存，美元指數維持高檔震盪，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險，並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7100

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在 102 附近盤整，帶領 USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。二、日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢，強調日本已脫離通縮，將完全尊重央行政策獨立性，暗示不反對進一步升息。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.10-159.50。 
前週交易區間 
157.41 158.50

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪走跌。週初，因法國債券風險與區域政治不確定性，
EUR/USD 下行最低來到 1.1160；週間，受中東地緣風險升溫與歐洲財政疑慮帶動避
險需求，加上美債殖利率維持高檔，美元利差優勢延續，歐元維持低檔；臨近週末，
逢低歐元有買盤支撐，帶動歐元收復部分跌幅，終場收在 1.1201，週線下跌 46 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學
家連恩表示，夏末能源成本飆升為經濟增長帶來下行風險，並可能限制央行收緊貨
幣政策的力道，與此同時，歐元區 10 月 Sentix 投資者信心指數由 5.1 降至 2.7，德
國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐，後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1100-1.1260 
前週交易區間 
1.1160 1.1276

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.05-115.10.08 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.05% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本週邁入新的提存期，央行連日減發存單，令市場資金供
給充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週一央行標售 364 天期定存單 1100 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.602%，低於
市場預期區間 1.61%-1.635%，較上期標售結果下跌 4.1 個基本點。 
週 四 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 受美債利率續創新高影響，週四 10 年期指標券 A15109 成交在 2.05%，創同
年期券自 2008 年以來高點，5 年期指標券 A15108 則未成交，最近成交利率
為 1.82%。 
台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨
警戒線，且創兩年半來最大漲幅，目前油價仍居高難下，通膨壓力將令央行維
持偏緊的貨幣政策，加上國際債市利率持續走升，台債殖利率恐面臨上行壓
力，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.95%，10 年券區間為 2.0%~2.10%。

敬鵬 (2355 TT)

內外皆美

調升投資評等至買進，目標價 82 元，上漲空間 49%。 
1. 3Q26 毛利率突破 20%，EPS1.34 元，優於預期。 
2. 漲價及台幣貶值雙重有利因子加持，估計 4Q26 季增雙位數。 
3. 泰國新廠新增 68.23 億泰銖投資案。

3Q26 毛利率突破 20%，EPS1.34 元，優於預期 
3Q26 合併營收 48.12 億元，QOQ+13.5%，YOY+24.7%，，毛利率 20.5%，較前
一季 12.2%及同期 8.6%分別大增 8.3 及 11.9ppt，大幅優於市場共識，而毛利率提
升主要是順利反應原料成本及產品組合優化等，另匯兌利益挹注近一億元，單季稅
後淨利 5.33 億元，QOQ+196.6%、YOY+617.9%，EPS 1.34 元，表現優於預期。
累計前三季稅後淨利 9.6 億元，YOY+152.6%，EPS 2.42 元。

漲價及台幣貶值雙重有利因子加持，估計 4Q26 季增雙位數 
展望 4Q26，AI 產能供應不求外溢效益持續顯現，加上原料緊缺，下游車用客戶
漲價接受度提升，公司表示目前訂單需求遠大於供給，而營收及成長力道視
CCL、PP、鑽針..等原料供應狀況，現在主要瓶頸就是缺料。考量產品價格調漲及
近期台幣貶值有利因子，富邦估計季營收成長 16.5%，毛利率向上，單季
EPS1.73 元，QOQ+29.3%，YOY+171.2%。

泰國新廠新增 68.23 億元泰銖投資案 
泰國新廠預計 2026 年底前完工，預計 1H27 產出貢獻，公司表示該廠非單一客戶
專廠，產品應用也非單一。另因應未來營運發展需求，公司近日泰國廠董事會也通
過 68.23 億元泰銖投資案，預計 2026 年到 2029 年進行產能擴充的設備購置計劃。

富邦不同之處 
富邦為少數持續追蹤且出具更新報告券商之一。

評價與風險 
季報表現大幅優於市場預期，展望後市，受惠價格調漲順利及台幣貶值效益，營運
獲利向上，以 2027 年的 8.1 倍 P/E 評價來看並不高，因此我們調升投資評等至買
進，目標價 82 元，約相當 2027 年的 12 倍 P/E。

富邦的股票評等標準 
評等  定義 
買進 預估未來6個月內的絕對報酬超過15%  
中立  預估未來6個月內有絕對報酬介於15%與負15%之間 
賣出 預估未來6個月內的絕對報酬高於負15% 
未評等 由於富邦目前與該公司有特定交易或沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等 
評估中 目前正在研議個股的投資評等，將於3到6個月內提供投資評等

產業評等 定義 
優於大盤 預估該產業在未來6個月內會比大盤指數表現突出 
持平 預估該產業在未來6個月內與大盤指數表現相較持平 
劣於大盤 預估該產業在未來6個月內會比大盤指數表現較差

Fubon's Rating System 
Rating Definition 
Buy Expected absolute return to be over 15% within the next 6 months 
Neutral  Expected absolute return to be level within the next 6 months 
Sell  Expected absolute return to be over negative 15% within the next 6 months 
Not Rated (NR)  Pursuant to Fubon acting in deals involving the company or there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an investment rating 
Under Review Fubon is in the process of determining an investment rating and will assign a rating within the 3- to 6-month horizon

Sector Rating Definition 
Overweight Sector expected to outperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months 
Market Weight  Sector expected to perform in line with the broader market averages within the next 6-12 months 
Underweight  Sector expected to underperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months.

### Page 3

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 3
A Closer Look at the Fundamentals
Why we changed our minds – March caution, May upside
surprise
We downgraded Winbond and Nanya Tech in March (Old Memory: Turning Selective) as
elevated DDR4 pricing encouraged supply responses and customer pushback, pointing to
narrower shortages and moderating price hikes. In May, we reversed those calls as lower
expected supply from Micron and Hynix, alongside stronger server and enterprise SSD

demand, widened the projected supply-demand gap (Old Memory: Upside Surprise
Ahead). The distinction matters: March was a selective DDR4 call; today, we see a broader
challenge to old memory’s pricing durability.
What has changed
Pricing power cannot offset weaker demand indefinitely
Our constructive thesis was that niche memory, with limited fixed-price LTA constraints,
could reprice faster and outperform mainstream memory. We now see that flexibility

becoming a two-way risk. Rising uncertainty over cloud spending weakens confidence in
incremental enterprise demand, while much weaker consumer demand challenges
customers’ ability to absorb further hikes.
After the US midterms, our US team now expects ~20% of 2028e US
data center capacity additions to shift to 2029
This include some projects in Texas and Ohio, as governors introduce new data center
legislation (refer to Buy the Drop: AI Investment Opportunities Post the US Midterms by

Stephen Byrd). Stephen has shifted ~5GW of US data center capacity additions from 2028
to 2029  ( Exhibit 11 ) – equivalent to 20% of US and 10% of global 2028 capacity
additions. This implies a 4% Y/Y decline in US data center GW additions  in 2028 before a
38% Y/Y rise in 2029. We now expect global data center additions to increase 9% in 2028
and    20% Y/Y  in 2029.
This could create an air pocket across the tech supply chain in 2028,
potentially reducing pricing power and leading to an inventory build

Our latest PC semis work highlights procurement pull-ins in 1Q–3Q26, followed by a
deeper correction in 4Q, reinforcing the risk that earlier orders overstated underlying
consumption (PC Semis: More Divergent Outlooks).
Supply-demand update –  looking beyond the shortage
Our revised models for SLC NAND, MLC NAND, NOR, and DDR4 point to progressively
more balanced markets, particularly into 2028. We now expect 2028 supply-demand
deficits of -3%, -5%, and -1% for SLC NAND, MLC NAND, NOR, with a +1% excess for DDR4.

Near-term tightness need not disappear immediately for stocks to de-rate: a narrowing
shortage can reduce incremental pricing power before earnings peak. We therefore place

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
less weight on extrapolating recent ASP gains and more on normalized demand and
supply responses and sustainable profitability.

### Page 5

Idea

Morgan Stanley Research 11
Winbond: Estimate Revisions and Quarterly Financials
We adjust our EPS estimates +8% for 2026, -9% for 2027, and -22% for 2028:  The
2026 EPS increase reflects 3Q26 actual results. We also factor in a potential peak in
pricing power across NOR, SLC, and DDR4. Compared to the previous estimate, we now
reduce our flash wafer ASP assumptions throughout 2027-2028 and  26% into 4Q28, and
reduce our DRAM ASP per bit estimates throughout 2027-2028 and  23% into 4Q28,

without changing our flash or DRAM shipment estimates. Apart from reduced revenue
estimates, we also lower our gross margin estimates to account for the reduced ASP
estimates. Our changes in net  income/EPS estimates are largely a function of changes in
our revenue and gross profit estimates. We believe it is a good time to make these
changes now as a result of the narrower shortages, moderating price hikes, and
potentially delays in 2028 US cloud capex.

Winbond: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target to  NT$120
We continue to apply P/B multiple methodology to derive our price target, in line with our
approach for Greater China memory IDM peers and in view of the industry's high earnings
volatility. Our price target (base case scenario value) is set at 1.2x 2027e BVPS vs. our
prior target multiple of 2.9x 2027e BVPS, to reflect the potential peak in pricing power.
Our 1.2x target P/B is above our 1.0x P/B valuation anchor –  we still favor the company's

CUBE and S-SiCap businesses for their long-term potential. We see 1.0x P/B as the
downside valuation anchor because book value provides a psychological support level for
capital-intensive IDM/foundry stocks during cyclical downturns. When earnings are
temporarily depressed, investors typically place greater weight on the underlying net
asset base.
Our base case value/price target cut of 58% is almost entirely a function  of reduced target
multiple, rather than change in 2027e BVPS, which declines only 2% in our new estimate.

Our bull case value is reduced from NT$342 to NT$180, and our bear case value is
reduced from NT$118 to NT$50, based on 1.8x and 0.5x 2027e BVPS, respectively
(previously 3.4x and 1.2x).
We downgrade Winbond to EW
We continue to like the CUBE and S-SiCap businesses for longer-term potential, but we
see near-term pressure from NOR, SLC NAND, and DDR4 businesses, in all of which
industry supply is ramping faster than demand is increasing, especially as global cloud

capex may face partial delay from 2028 to 2029. We believe the stock still has strong
valuation support at 1.0-1.2x P/B, and therefore we believe an EW rating is fair.

GigaDevice: Estimate Revisions and Quarterly Financials
We cut our EPS estimates  25% for 2026, 36% for 2027, and 33% for 2028:  The 2026e
EPS change mainly reflects actual 2Q26 and 3Q26 results. The 2027e and 2028e EPS
changes reflect a potential peak in pricing power across DDR4, NOR Flash, and SLC NAND.
We now reduce our DRAM and others revenue estimates throughout 2027-2028 and 43%
into 4Q28, mainly reflecting changes in our DDR4 ASP estimates. We also reduce our

Flash (including NOR and SLC NAND) ASP estimates throughout 2027-2028 and  25% into
4Q28, without changing our Flash shipment estimates. We did not materially change our
GM assumptions. Our changes in net  income/EPS estimates are largely a function of
changes in our revenue and gross profit estimates. We believe it is a good time to make
these changes now as a result of the narrower shortages, moderating price hikes, and
potential delay in 2028 US cloud capex.

GigaDevice: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target  to Rmb259
We continue to derive our price target, which is our base case value, from a residual
income model. We believe this best captures the stock’s long-term value.
Our base case value cut of 65% can be attributed ~33% to earnings estimate revisions, as
has been elaborated, and ~33% from multiple compression, driven by intermediate growth
rate cut and beta increase.  (Note: the overall price target change is calculated from the

fully revised RI model. The individual contribution estimates are illustrative and should
not be added together or compounded to derive the overall change.).
We cut our intermediate growth rate to 8.0% from 11.3% to reflect stronger changes in
the supply environment compared to the end market demand over the medium to long
term. The potential peak in pricing power for niche memory (DDR4, SLC, MLC, NOR) and
the potential delay of 2028 US cloud capex now suggest slower revenue growth for

GigaDevice from 2026 to 2036. We think a 3.3ppt p.a. growth rate cut reflects the new
supply/demand dynamics with a closer gap.
We also raise our beta to 1.50 from 1.14, leading our cost of equity assumption to rise to
11.8% from 9.4%. This  reflects a cautious valuation scenario, to account for the recent
memory segment volatility, 70%+ correction from the stock price peak in June, and the
fabless model, which does not provide a floor support for valuation similar to the IDM

model. The elevated beta reflects the company's exposure to rapidly changing niche
memory pricing dynamics that could severely affect earnings with a downward bias
compared to our financial forecasts.
Our other key assumptions remain unchanged, including equity risk premium of 6.5%, risk-
free rate of 2% and payout ratio of 40%.
Our bull case value falls to Rmb448 from Rmb1,300, and our bear case value to Rmb112
from Rmb324.
We downgrade GigaDevice to EW

We still see the company as a domestic leader in MCU localization and believe its
customized DRAM business could show a revenue ramp over the next 1-2 years. We also
still like its strategic partnership with CXMT, which provides both capacity support and
process tech improvements. However, we see near-term pressure from the NOR, SLC
NAND, and DDR4 businesses, in all of which industry supply is ramping faster than
demand increase, especially as global cloud capex may face partial delay from 2028 to

2029. Fabless design houses do not face depreciation and utilization issues during a down-
cycle, but at the same time they lack a similar valuation anchor as IDM/foundry players
from their book value, and therefore we see potentially continued high volatility ahead.
Our new price target implies a 2027e P/E of 9x, below the historical average of 38x since
2016, owing to changes in pricing power.

PSMC: Earnings Estimate     Revisions
We    adjust our EPS forecasts +10% for 2026 and -11% each for 2027 and 2028:      The
2026e EPS change mainly reflects actual 2Q26 and 3Q26 results. The 2027e and 2028e
EPS changes reflect a potential peak in pricing power across DDR3/3 and SLC NAND. We
now reduce our DRAM wafer ASP estimates throughout 2027-2028 and 19% into 4Q28,
without changing our DRAM wafer shipment estimates. We did not materially change our

GM assumptions. Our changes in net  income/EPS estimates are largely a function of
changes in our revenue and gross profit estimates. We believe it is a good time to make
these changes now as a result of the narrower shortages, moderating price hikes, and
potential delay in 2028 US cloud capex.

PSMC: Valuation     Methodology
Our price target  is cut to NT$54
We incorporate our revised estimates and maintain our P/B valuation methodology. Our
new target, which is our base case value,  is based on a 2027e P/B of 1.0x, down from 2.0x
previously –  below the historical average of 1.5x since 2021, to reflect pricing
uncertainties.
Our base case value/price target cut of 51% is almost entirely a result of target multiple
reduction, rather than change in 2027e BVPS, which declined only 1.5% in our new

estimate. For PSMC, we use 1.0x P/B as the downside valuation anchor –  book value
provides a psychological support level for capital-intensive IDM/foundry stocks during
cyclical downturns. When earnings are temporarily depressed, investors typically place
greater weight on the underlying net asset base.
We decrease our bull case value from NT$177 to NT$86 and our bear case value from NT
$55 to NT$27, implying 1.6x and 0.5x 2027e P/B, respectively.

We downgrade PSMC to EW
We are positive on the company's emerging 3D AI foundry business, and we believe PSMC
is well positioned to benefit from its Micron partnership, including front-end migration to
the one-year  process node and back-end opportunities in advanced DRAM production
(e.g., HBM). The company is also catching up with the DRAM pricing up-cycle.
At the same time, its logic foundry business is set to enjoy continued price hikes into 2027.

However, on its memory foundry side, we see near-term pressure from DDR3/4, in which
industry supply is ramping faster than demand is increasing, especially as global cloud
capex may face partial delay from 2028 to 2029.
We believe the stock still has strong valuation support at 1.0x P/B, and therefore we
believe an EW rating is fair.

Macronix: Earnings  Estimate Revisions
We cut our 2026, 2027, and 2028 EPS forecasts 14%, 20%, and 21%, respectively: The
2026e EPS change mainly reflects actual 3Q26 results. The 2027e and 2028e EPS
changes reflect a potential peak in pricing power across NOR and MLC/SLC NAND. We
now reduce our NAND and /NOR wafer ASP estimates throughout 2027-2028 and 15%
and 8% into 4Q28 respectively, without changing our wafer shipment estimates. We did

not materially change our GM assumptions. Our changes in net  income/EPS estimates are
largely a function of changes in our revenue and gross profit estimates. We believe it is a
good time to make these changes now as a result of the narrower shortages, moderating
price hikes, and potential delay in 2028 US cloud capex.

Macronix: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target to NT$90 from NT$220
We continue to use a P/B multiple methodology to derive our price target, which is our
base case value, in line with our approach for Greater China memory IDM peers and in
view of the industry's high volatility. Our new target is based on a 2027e P/B of 1.0x, down
from 2.1x previously – below the average of 1.5x since 2017, given the potential peak in
pricing power for NOR and SLC/MLC NAND. For Macronix, we now use 1.0x P/B as the

downside valuation anchor because book value provides a psychological support level for
capital-intensive IDM/foundry stocks during cyclical downturns. When earnings are
temporarily depressed, investors typically place greater weight on the underlying net
asset base.
Our base case value/price target cut of 59% reflects both target multiple reduction and a
15% 2027 BVPS estimate reduction (relatively large because of a smaller base).
We cut our bull case value from NT$355 to NT$145, which is 1.6x our 2027e BVPS. We cut

our bear case value from NT$100 to NT$41, which is 0.5x our 2027e BVPS.
We downgrade Macronix to EW
We continue to like its unique position in MLC NAND as the only major supplier still
ramping up capacity. However, we do see near-term pressure from NOR, DDR3/4, and
even SLC/MLC NAND, in all of which  industry supply is ramping faster than demand is
increasing, especially as global cloud capex may face partial delay from 2028 to 2029. We
believe the stock still has strong valuation support at 1.0x P/B, and therefore we believe

an EW rating is fair.

Nanya Tech: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target from NT$600 to NT$550
Our price target (base case scenario value) reflects a target BVPS multiple of 2.04x for
2027 (was 4.47x) versus ROE of 59% for 2027.  We lower our target multiple to reflect
potential peak in DDR4 pricing power and recent memory segment volatility. For Nanya
Tech, our new base case target P/B multiple of 2.04x P/B is stronger than our downside

valuation anchor of 1.0x P/B for capital-intensive IDM/foundry stocks.  This is because we
expect Nanya to remain relatively resilient compared to other DDR4 players in our
coverage, given stronger process technology and potential DDR5 migration.
Our base case value/price target cut of 8% reflects both target multiple reduction and
BVPS estimate adjustment of 3% arising from earnings estimate revisions.
We cut our bull and bear case values from NT$1,270 and NT$330 to NT$1,150 and NT

$300, respectively -- 4.27x and 1.12x 2027e BVPS.
We remain OW
Despite potentially peaking pricing power seen in DDR4, we remain positive on DDR5 in
view of continued strong demand from servers. Nanya is already producing DDR5 on its
second-generation 10-nm process, with DDR5 contributing ~10% of revenue; its next
process enters customer qualification in 1Q27, and EUV implementation is targeted for
2028. This establishes its edge in process technology vs the other DDR4 vendors in our

coverage and a stronger position in DDR5 migration. Valuation is also attractive, in our
view. Therefore, we remain OW on Nanya Tech.

AP Memory: Estimate revisions summary
We  adjust our EPS estimates  +21% for 2026, +2% for 2027, and -12% for 2028: We
factor in 3Q26 preliminary results. We adjust our 2028 forecasts  mainly to account for
supply chain risks. We note that the company's IoTRAM is less affected by  volatility of
typical commodity DRAM pricing. The 2027e and 2028e EPS changes mainly reflect
supply chain uncertainties around S-SiCap. We now reduce our S-SiCap unit shipment

estimates throughout 2027-2028 and  50%+ into 4Q28. We also slightly reduced  our IoT
RAM ASP estimates throughout 2027-28 to reflect peaking DDR3/4 pricing power. In
addition, we reduced our GM assumptions across product lines to reflect potential  peak in
pricing power and supply chain uncertainties. Our changes in net  income/EPS estimates
are largely a function of changes in our revenue and gross profit estimates. We believe it is
a good time to make these changes now as a result of the narrower shortages, moderating

price hikes, and potential delay in 2028 US cloud capex.

Phison: Estimate Revisions
We adjust our 2026, 2027, and 2028 EPS estimates: We factor in 3Q26 results, which
demonstrated continued revenue growth (+27% Q/Q) but also gross margin decline
(59.7% in 3Q, down from 65.3% in 2Q). In 4Q26 and 2027, we expect emerging weakness
from consumer tech and a more moderate pricing environment to result in 4Q revenue
decline, with GM starting to normalize. We think normalized GM is likely to extend into

2028.
We present further discussion of our EPS estimates under Valuation Methodology.

Phison: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target from NT$2,588 to NT$1,357
Our price target is our base case value, derived from a residual income (RI) model. Our cut
reflects our latest earnings estimates. We also cut our terminal growth rate assumption
from 4.0% to 3.0% to be broadly in line with a conservative estimate of long-term
nominal economic growth in developed economies. We believe this is appropriate for

Phison because rising storage demand should support continued growth, while declining
NAND prices per bit, competition, and maturing SSD penetration should limit the
conversion of volume growth into earnings growth, bringing its growth closer to that of
the broader economy over time.
Our other RI parameters are unchanged, including a cost of equity of 9.0%, a medium-
term growth rate of 9.0%, and a dividend payout ratio assumption of 50.0%.
Apart from the earnings estimate revisions and terminal growth rate change, a material

part of the price target change is driven by an error in the previously published residual
income model (August 2026). The error has now been corrected. In the previous RI model,
the 2028e net income was mistakenly calculated as 2027e net income * (1+ intermediate
growth rate), where it should have been linked to the actual 2028e net income forecast.
This overstated the previous price target.
Our earnings estimates have also changed materially  from the previously published

version: they increase 12% for 2026, 60% for 2027, and 37% for  2028. This mainly reflects
higher GM assumptions of 61.2%, 51.9%, and 42.0%, respectively; previously we assumed
60.1%, 43.4%, and 39.5%.
Our base case value/price target is lowered 48%, of which roughly +37% is contributed by
earnings estimate revisions, -6% is contributed by terminal growth rate reduction, and -
83% is contributed by the correction of the prior RI model error. (The overall price target

change is calculated from the fully revised RI model. The individual contribution estimates
are illustrative and should not be added together or compounded to derive the overall
change.)
We cut our bull and bear case values from NT$3,400 and NT$1,250 to NT$1,782 and NT
$655, respectively, implying 8.4x 2027e EPS in the bull case and 3.1x 2027e EPS in the
bear case.
We downgrade Phison to UW
The company hopes its AiDaptive module and service business can pick up and offset the

margin pressure in the conventional module business in the coming quarters, which we
believe is worth monitoring. However, its 3Q GM  of 59% missed the Street estimate of
60.3%; we see this as a potential sign that the previously strong margin profile supported
by low-cost inventory may start to face more downside. Specifically, the AI storage part of
its business (eSSD, and cSSD for AI PC, together 40% of revenue) may face margin
decline. This is what we have been cautious about, and we see the 3Q margin miss as

having provided a good opportunity to downgrade this stock, which we view as

### Page 46

M
Idea
46
overvalued based on our revised assumptions.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

股價相對大盤走勢

大量(3167 TT) Buy

CPO 設備將於 3Q27 量產，給 Buy 的投資建議。 
投資建議：大量受惠於 AI 帶動 PCB 製造、半導體檢測需求，
獲利大幅成長，CPO 設備將於 3Q27 量產，給 Buy 的投資建議。 
大量受惠於 AI 伺服器帶動高階 PCB 鑽孔設備：大量在±2 mil 
PCB 背鑽計量領域的地位依然穩固；大量受惠於 AI 伺服器帶
動高階 PCB 鑽孔設備需求的趨勢，而非單純受惠某一客戶的拉
貨。 
3Q26 毛利率季增約 6pts： 大量 3Q26 營收 36.71 億元，
QoQ+28.40%；稅後純益 8.99 億元，QoQ+65.51%；稅後 EPS 9.85
元。 
3Q26 毛利率季增約 6pts，主要來自產品組合優化，包含高階
CCD 機種及 TM3、 TM4。一般機種毛利率約 20~30%，高階機
種毛利率約 50%， TM3、 TM4 毛利率更高，TM4 於 3Q26 出貨
約 5 台。 
大量的子公司-大量先進智能規劃 IPO：大量先進智能已經於
08/01/2026 獨立，目前大量持股大量先進智能 100%，預計大量
先進智能上櫃後，大量持股大量先進智能仍會超過 60%。  
大量先進智能的核心技術包含了半導體的精密量測
(Metrology)、 AOI 智慧檢測(Inspection)及 (Automation)自動化。
產品應用包含 CoWoS、 SoIC 及面板級封裝(FOPLP)(CoPoS)、
CPO 等。 
群益預估大量 2026 年 /2027 年稅後 EPS 30.43 元 /62.00 元：群
益預估大量 2026 年 /2027 年營收為 121.87 億元/182.81 億元，

YoY+287.95%/+103.72%；稅後 EPS 30.43 元 /62.00 元。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
E：(Environmental)。大量科技秉持「愛護地球、永續發展」之理念，持續推
動環境管理制度，並透過日常營運管理落實環境保護與污染預防，降低營運
活動對環境造成之影響。大量科技秉持「愛護地球、永續發展」之理念，持
續推動環境管理制度，並透過日常營運管理落實環境保護與污染預防，降低
營運活動對環境造成之影響。大量科技針對廠區內原物料、製程、生產活動
及服務建立環境考量評估機制，評估範圍涵蓋正常、異常及緊急狀況下可能
產生之環境影響，並納入空氣排放、水污染、廢棄物、能源使用、土地污染、
噪音及其他環境衝擊等項目。 
S：(Social)。大量科技重視人才發展與員工照顧，將員工視為公司穩健營運
與永續經營的重要基礎。大量科技秉持以人為本的精神，致力於培育與留任
專業人才，並透過多元招募、薪酬福利、教育訓練、勞資溝通及權益保障等
制度，持續打造友善、平等且具支持性的職場環境。大量科技於人才任用上
遵循相關勞動法令及多元平等原則，重視員工專業能力發展及工作權益保
障，並持續透過教育訓練提升員工技能，強化員工與公司共同成長之基礎。
G：(Governance)。大量科技致力於推動產品朝高規格、高自動化、高智能化
及完全整合化之方向發展，以貼近客戶需求，並與客戶共同開發新產品。此
外，公司持續投入自動化設備之研發，厚植核心技術能力，並持續開發進階
產品，以掌握市場先機。為確保制度有效運作，大量科技於企劃室下設企業
誠信經營小組，由總經理擔任召集人，負責誠信經營政策之推動與執行情形
之監督，並每年向董事會報告執行成果及防範不誠信行為之措施。大量科技
依董事會通過之「誠信經營守則」及相關內部規章，建立對於不誠信行為之
相關防範機制，並透過內部控制、檢舉制度及教育訓練等措施，確保誠信經
營政策之落實。 
公司簡介： 
大量的產主要產品應用於製造、半導體先進製程與先進封裝，以及光電、玻
璃基板與精密材料加工等領域。透過自主研發之控制器、應用軟體、視覺定
位、深度控制、運動控制及量測檢測技術，大量科技依客戶製程需求提供設
備及技術服務。 
PCB 設備：PCB 鑽孔定位、PCB 成型切割、邊緣滾塗、針塗等塗佈製程及
高速型內層銅深度量測機等。 
半導體設備：CMP Pad 即時量測監控設備、3D IC 先進封裝製程晶圓及晶片
量測設備、FOPLP 形貌量測設備、3DIC 先進封裝製程檢測設備、Flip Chip 
製程檢測設備、捲帶包裝晶片本體及外觀缺陷之檢查設備等、封裝製程自動
化設備等。 
大量 PCB 客戶的客戶包含台灣、中國的 PCB 廠；半導體設備客戶包含晶圓
代工廠、OSAT。  
高階背鑽設備的市場，主要同業為德國 Schmoll Maschinen。  
資料來源：大量年報。

4 群益投顧 
PCB 設備包含鑽孔機、成型機和塗膠設備。鑽孔設備也包含量測設備(TM3、
TM4 系列，應用於背鑽檢測)。  
大量有找外包廠商來增加產能： 
考量標準產品與高階產品，大量目前自有的月產能約 400 台，預計到 2027
年每月會達 500 台，大量會再增加外包的產能約 100~150 台。 
大量的子公司-大量先進智能規劃 IPO：  
大量先進智能已經於 08/01/2026 獨立，目前大量持股大量先進智能 100%，
預計大量先進智能未來上櫃後，大量持股大量先進智能仍會超過 60%。  
大量先進智能的核心技術包含了半導體的精密量測(Metrology)、AOI 智慧檢
測 (Inspection)及 (Automation)自動化。產品應用包含 CoWoS、 SoIC 及面板
級封裝(FOPLP)(CoPoS)、 CPO 等。

資料來源：大量法說簡報。 
2027 年 PCB 與半導體設備展望皆正向： 
PCB 設備 2027 年每月都有要交機，交期約 5~6 月；半導體設備 B/B ratio 維
持在 2 以上。 
大量預估 2027 年 CCD 成型機與鑽孔機將維持雙位數成長，TM3 完成階段
性任務後出貨將減少，TM4 出貨量預期會有大幅倍數成長。PCB 在手訂單
有達千台。

5 群益投顧 
大量有 CPO 相關設備，單價較一般半導體設備高： 
大量目前與北美 Tier 1 客戶合作，2026 年預計完成第一條產線的建置，量
產時程預計落在 3Q27。 2027 年設備需求量將倍數成長。 
由於該設備為整合光學前處理、檢測、後處理的一體機，結合了 3~4 項關鍵
技術，平均售價(ASP)約為一般晶圓檢測設備的 300~400%。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

기아 (000270)

26.3Q Review: 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요  Company Brief

26.3Q Review: 매출액 31.6조원, 영업이익 1.8조원  
기아는 26.3Q 매출액 31.6조원 (+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원 
(+24.3% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, +5.8% OPM) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 26.3Q 영업이익은 
시장 컨센서스 (2.36조원)를 22.9% 하회했다 [iM증권 추정치: 매출액 31.5조원, 
영업이익 2.3조원]. 주요 구성 요소별 내용은 아래와 같다. 
① P: 26.3Q 26.3Q 전동화 차량 판매는 30.0만대 (+47% YoY, 판매 비중 
36.2%)를 기록했는데, 이 중 HEV 17.2만대 (+46% YoY), EV 12.3만대 
(+75% YoY)로 구성됨. 미국 HEV 판매가 전년 동기 대비 118% 증가하며 
시장 성장률을 약 4배 상회했고, 특히 서유럽 EV 판매가 전년 대비 163% 
증가함. 단 26.3Q 중 원화가 빠르게 강세로 전환되며 원/달러, 원/유로 
평균 환율은 전분기 대비 각 5.2%, 6.2% 하락함에 따른 – 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매는 84.7만대 (+7.9% YoY)를 기록했고, 북미, 유럽, 
인도 등 주요 시장에서 고른 성장 나타남. 26.3Q 소매 판매 또한 82.9만대 
(+6.9% YoY)로 글로벌 산업 수요 (-6% YoY)를 상회. 북미는 HEV, 내수와 
유럽은 EV, 인도는 신형 셀토스와 소넷을 중심으로 판매가 확대됨 (이하 
26.3Q 권역별 도매 판매 YoY, 국내: +2.2%, 북미: +7.6%. 유럽: +19.3%. 
인도: +38.5%). 이란 사태 장기화로 아중동 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 
28.4% 감소했으나, 수요가 견조한 중남미와 CIS로 물량을 전환하며 
감소분을 상당 부분 만회함. 연간 도매 판매 가이던스 (335만대)를 유지함. 
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 약 0.7조원으로 추정되며, 전년 동기 대비 
5,000억원 감소함 (25.2Q 0.7조원, 25.3Q 1.2조원, 25.4Q 1.0조원, 26.1Q 
0.8조원). 반면 인센티브 증가 및 가격 인하 7,270억원, 서유럽 EV 비중 
확대에 따른 믹스 악화 4,650억원, 원자재 가격 상승 영향 3,680억원 등 
총 1.6조원의 감익 요인이 발생함. 이에 26.3Q 매출원가율은 84.1%로 
전년 동기 대비 3.0%p 상승함 (인센티브 +1.9%p, 재료비 +1.2%p). 다만 
26.3Q 기말 환율 급락하며 판매보증충당부채 환입 규모가 축소되었고 
판매보증비 약 9,000억원 감소. 판매관리비율은 10.1%로 3.7%p 개선됨. 
26.3Q 기아 순이익은 1.29조원으로 전년 동기 대비 9.6% 감소했다. 원화 강세에 
따라 외환환산 관련 이익이 전년 동기 540억원 이익에서 3,718억원으로 손실 
전환됐고, 현대모비스 등 관계사 이익 감소로 지분법손익 또한 360억원 감소했기 
때문이다 (현대차 순이익 감소 논리와 동일). 1) 산업 수요 감소와 중동 판매 차질에도 
권역별 파워트레인 대응을 통해 그룹사 대비 가지고 있는 외형 성장성을 증명했으나, 
2) 유럽 EV 판매 확대 과정에서 발생한 인센티브와 믹스 악화라는 비용 부담, 원자재 
가격 상승이 관세 감소 효과를 대부분 반납했고, 3) 분기 중 급격한 원화 강세가 
더해지며 시장 기대를 크게 하회하는 수익성을 기록했다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다.

2

실적 발표 후 주가 급락: 성장에도 불구하고 수익성에 대한 우려  
26.10.30 실적 발표 당일 기아 주가는 11.7% 하락했다. 하루 전 발표된 그룹사 실적 
발표와 상반된 모습이었다. 시장이 이번 실적에서 확인한 우려는 아래와 같다. 첫째, 
성장과 이익의 괴리다. 관세 영향을 제외한 26.3Q 영업이익은 2.52조원으로 전년 
동기(2.70조원) 대비 오히려 1,770억원 감소했다. 판매 증가에 따른 물량 효과 
(+4,420억원)와 환율 효과 (+5,290억원)가 발생했음에도, 인센티브와 믹스 악화로 이를 
대부분 반납한 것이다. 기아의 외형 성장 상당 부분이 유럽 EV 판매 증가에서 나오는데, 
해당 시장에서는 중국 EV 업체와의 경쟁으로 공격적인 프로모션이 불가피하다. 26.3Q 
유럽 권역에서의 중소형 EV 판매가 사업 계획을 크게 웃돌며 EV 믹스는 13.1%에서 
14.8%로 확대됐다. 시장은 이를 M/S를 위해 수익성을 훼손하고 있다고 받아들이는 
것이다. 둘째, 당장 26.4Q에도 유의미한 수익성 개선이 없을 것이라는 점이다. 기아는 
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 4Q 영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이며, 연초 
제시한 연간 가이던스 (영업이익 10.2조원, +8.3% OPM)에 다소 미달할 것이라고 
밝혔다. 중동 분쟁의 영향이 최소 연말까지 이어지고, 원자재와 물류비 부담 또한 
지속될 것이 회사의 판단이다. 무엇보다 원/달러 환율이 26.2Q 평균 약 1,505원에서 
26.3Q 약 1,430원, 현재 1,320~1,340원 수준까지 하락하며 이에 따른 - 영향이 커질 
수 있다. 셋째, 그룹사와의 이익 체력 비교다. 현대차는 26.3Q 파업과 생산 차질로 도매 
판매가 11.3% 감소했음에도 원가 절감을 통해 영업이익이 5.5% 증가했고, 시장 
기대를 상회했다. 반면 기아는 판매가 7.9% 증가했음에도 영업이익이 컨센서스를 20% 
이상 하회했다. 26.3Q 양사 영업이익 격차는 약 8,600억원까지 벌어진 상황이다.  
단기 노이즈를 제외한다면 기아의 기업 가치는 여전히 높다  
그럼에도 당사는 기아의 EV 전략, 낮은 수익성을 이유로 중장기 기업 가치에 대한 
기대를 낮출 이유는 없다고 판단한다. 첫째, 외부 요인을 걷어내면 본원적인 수익 
창출력은 유지되고 있다. 기아는 26.3Q 영업이익률을 낮춘 외부 요인을 원자재 1.2%p, 
중동 판매 차질 0.7%p, 환율 1.7%p 등 총 3.6%p로 분석했다. 즉 중동 분쟁이 
해소되면 상당 부분이 사라질 일회성 요인이다. 둘째, EV 수익성 개선 경로는 HEV가 
걸어온 길과 같다. 기아는 HEV 역시 도입 초기에는 수익을 내지 못했으나 현재는 ICE에 
버금가는 수익성을 내고 있다며, 26~27년 현재의 EV 전략을 유지하겠다고 밝혔다. 
유럽 ICE 시장은 26.3Q 전년 동기 대비 약 20% 축소된 반면 EV 시장은 50% 이상 
성장하고 있다. 전환기에 스케일을 확보하지 못하면 EV 마진을 개선할 방법도 없다. 
기아는 향후 3년간 배터리, 모터, 히트펌프 등 주요 부품에서 연 10% 수준의 원가 
절감을 추진하고, 2~3년 내 EV를 HEV와 같은 수익성 경로에 올려놓겠다는 계획이다. 
셋째, 중장기 성장 계획을 수정하지 않았다. 기아는 27년 사업 계획이 CID 당시 제시한 
숫자와 큰 차이가 없을 것이며, 중장기 영업이익률 10% 목표도 유지했다. IAA 시행에 
대비해 슬로바키아 공장에서 EV를 전량 생산하고 ICE 국내 수출 방안도 검토 중이다. 
주주환원 측면에서도 TSR 35%를 지키고, 자사주 매입보다 배당을 우선하겠다는 
입장이다. 10/30 종가와 최소 DPS 기준만으로도 배당수익률은 6.3%에 달하고 있다.

기아 판매관리비 (좌) 판매관리비율 (우)
  26.4Q 기아 매출액 31.8조원, 영업이익 2.2조원 전망 
기아 26.4Q 실적을 매출액 31.8조원 (+13.2% YoY), 영업이익 2.2조원 (+20.8% YoY, 
+7.0% OPM)으로 전망한다. 26.4Q 판매 성장세는 이어지고, 3Q에 집중 반영된 원자재 
부담 일부 완화와 계절적 성수기 효과로 3Q 대비 수익성은 소폭 개선될 것이다. 다만 
1) 원화 강세에 따른 평균 환율 하락, 2) 유럽 EV 인센티브의 높은 수준 유지 (미국은 
할부 금리 상승으로 QoQ 약 200달러 증가, 유럽은 전분기와 유사), 3) 중동 판매 
차질과 원자재, 물류비 부담 지속을 감안하면 의미 있는 반등은 어렵다. 이는 4Q 
영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이라는 회사의 언급과도 궤를 같이한다. 
기아 목표주가 150,000원으로 하향  
기아 목표주가를 26.4Q 추정치 하향을 반영해 150,000원으로 하향한다. 기아의 판매 
경쟁력은 금번 분기에도 증명됐다. 다만 EV 판매 확대가 수익성 개선으로 연결된다는 
점이 숫자로 확인되기 전까지, 시장은 기아의 성장에 할인율을 적용할 것이다. 중동 
분쟁 해소와 EV 원가 절감이 맞물리는 27년이 기아가 수익성을 증명할 시점이 될 
것이며, 6%를 상회하는 배당수익률이 그때까지 주가의 하방을 지지할 것으로 판단된다.

 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및

Document 264: AI资本开支见顶=美元见顶？高盛2027展望：美元即将迎来“最后一涨”

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI资本开支见顶=美元见顶？高盛2027展望：美元即将迎来“最后一涨”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛认为，美元短期内仍有上⾏空间，但 2027 年将迎来实质性逆转。⽀撑这轮涨势的核⼼
驱动⼒正是 AI 相关投资预期的持续升温，⽽⼀旦这⼀预期⻅顶，美元也将⾯临同样的拐
点。
摘要：
美元与 AI 资本开⽀预期深度绑定，⾼盛警告：当 AI 叙事退潮，美元将随之承压。
⾼盛外汇策略团队在《 2027 全球外汇展望》报告中指出，美元短期内仍有上⾏空间，但 2027 年
将迎来实质性逆转。在历经 2025 年的⼤规模抛售与多元化配置后，美元于 2026 年悄然收复失地
—— 欧元兑美元回落⾄ " 解放⽇ " 以来低位，美元指数（ DXY ）触及 2025 年 4 ⽉以来最⾼点，美元
兑⽇元逼近 160 关⼝。⾼盛认为，⽀撑这轮涨势的核⼼驱动⼒正是 AI 相关投资预期的持续升温，
⽽⼀旦这⼀预期⻅顶，美元也将⾯临同样的拐点。
数据显⽰，外国投资者在截⾄今年 8 ⽉的⼀年间净购⼊创纪录的 1.47 万亿美元美国股票及企业债
券，⼏乎是全球⾦融危机前峰值的两倍。外资持有的美国股票中，约 90% 为账⾯浮盈⽽⾮实际

增量买⼊，⽽对冲⽐率处于历史低位 —— 换⾔之，仅对冲⽐率的结构性上移，就⾜以在⽆需⼤
规模资本外流的情况下对美元形成实质性压制。⾼盛警告， AI 资本开⽀预期⼀旦显现⻅顶迹
象，投资者可能要求更⼤的价格折让 —— 包括汇率层⾯的让步。
美元重回⾼估区间， AI 成为核⼼⽀撑变量
美元估值已重回历史偏⾼⽔平。⾼盛汇率错配模型显⽰，贸易加权美元较合理价值⾼估约
13% ，较 9 ⽉时的 7% ⼤幅上升；对亚洲货币的⾼估幅度则始终维持在 25% 左右，从未真正收
窄。
⾼盛策略师 Kamakshya Trivedi 与 Michael Cahill 在报告中明确将美元定性为 "AI 交易 " ，" 美元表
现与 AI 相关股票表现⾼度挂钩 —— ⽆论⾏情好坏皆然。 " 这⼀判断背后有数据⽀撑：在外国资本
流⼊美国的结构中，私⼈购买美国企业股票的⽐例已飙升⾄ 40 年历史区间的顶端。
值得注意的是， 2026 年外汇市场呈现出⼀个罕⻅悖论：全年外汇持仓变动幅度创历史之最，但
实际汇率波动区间却极为狭窄。⾼盛将这⼀现象归结为低衰退⻛险与有限政策分化并存的市场

环境，使得套息交易主导全年，⽅向性押注则普遍受挫。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

美联储鹰派⽴场为美元提供短期⽀撑
⾼盛并不建议当下即做空美元，原因在于美联储持续偏鹰的政策信号。在最新通胀数据显著超
预期 ——70 位预测⼈⼠中仅⼀位预测值更⾼ —— 后，⾼盛经济学家将今年加息次数上调⾄三
次。
10 年期美债收益率已重返 5% 上⽅，最新 PPI 数据创四年新⾼，均构成对美元的阶段性⽀撑。⾼
盛基准预测认为，通胀最终将趋于温和，美联储实际收紧幅度将远⼩于市场当前定价，届时美
元将承压。但该⾏坦承，近期⻛险仍 " 偏向美元⾛强 " ，尤其若美国⾦融条件收紧的外溢效应进⼀
步暴露欧洲脆弱性。
就欧元⽽⾔，⾼盛预测欧元兑美元将在三个⽉内跌⾄ 1.08 ，主因能源冲击带来的贸易条件恶化效
应滞后传导，以及法国上半年经济接近停滞（该预测低于市场普遍预期）；随后于 2027 年底反弹
⾄ 1.15 。⾼盛同时指出，法国的可信财政整固措施可能要等到 6 ⽉议会选举之后才能落地，⽽此
类措施往往需要市场压⼒作为政治推⼿。
⽇元：低估 30% ，政策转向提供结构性⽀撑
如果说美元是 AI 交易的多头，⽇元则是⾼盛设定的 AI 对冲⼯具。按照该⾏⾸选的公允价值模型，

⽇元对美元低估幅度约达 30% ，是 G10 货币中估值偏差最为显著的货币之⼀。
政策层⾯，⽇本央⾏的⽴场正在切实转变。⾼盛经济学家预计，⽇本央⾏将于 2027 年 6 ⽉前将政
策利率升⾄ 2% ，意味着还有三次 25 个基点的加息，⾼于市场⽬前约 1.75% 的隐含定价。该⾏还
注意到，⾃ 10 ⽉初以来，在纳斯达克跑输标普 500 指数逾 1% 的交易⽇内，⽇元平均涨幅约为
0.3% ，是 G10 表现最佳的货币。
⾼盛对美元兑⽇元的路径预测为：三个⽉ 158 、六个⽉ 155 、⼗⼆个⽉ 150 ，较当前约 160 的⽔平
下跌约 6% 。该⾏同时警⽰， " 若市场对 AI 预期的质疑更为显著，调整幅度可能⼤幅超出上述预
测 " 。
AI 债务泡沫与对冲⽐率是两⼤隐患
AI 叙事的脆弱性不仅体现在股票估值层⾯。
全球 AI 相关债务发⾏规模在 11 ⽉初已达 6870 亿美元，⽽超额流动性正在收缩，盈利上调动能也
在放缓。⾼盛估算， AI 投资约占美国实际 GDP 三季度同⽐增⻓的三分之⼆，⼀旦融资环境收

紧，负债最重的参与者将⾸当其冲，潜在冲击将远超科技⾏业范畴。
德意志银⾏策略师 George Saravelos 此前亦发出类似警告，指出外国持有的逾 20 万亿美元美国
股票中，约 90% 为账⾯价格升值⽽⾮实际买⼊，且整体对冲⽐率极低 —— 换⾔之，仅对冲⽐率
的结构性上移，就⾜以在⽆需⼤规模资本外流的情况下对美元形成实质性压制。
2027 年汇市格局：谁赢谁输
⾼盛 2027 年外汇总回报排名围绕四⼤主线展开：商品敏感货币随能源价格正常化⽽反弹、 AI 溢
出效应持续向亚洲货币传导、套息收益贡献收窄但依然为正、以及⽇元获得可持续政策⽀撑。
在预期总回报排名中，⾼息货币巴西雷亚尔（约 15% ）与南⾮兰特（约 10% ）位居前列；以⾊
列谢克尔、挪威克朗以及美元本⾝则处于末位。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

廉期权对冲尾部⻛险 " 具有较⾼性价⽐ —— ⽆论是美元超预期⾛强还是加速下跌的情景，当前的

（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ OPEC プラス有志７ヵ国は 11 月 1 日のオンライン会合で、12 月の原油生産目標を据え置き、
３ヵ月連続で増産を見送った。７ヵ国合計の生産目標は日量約 3,101 万バレルである。 
○ 据え置きの背景には、イラン情勢の緊迫化がある。ホルムズ海峡などで原油輸送に支障が生
じており、目標を引き上げても実際の供給増にはつながりにくい。ＩＥＡによれば、９月の
湾岸産油国の原油輸出量は日量 1,390 万バレルと、前月から増えたものの緊迫化前の約４分
の３にとどまる。石油製品やＬＰＧの輸出は戦前の半分以下にとどまるほか、10 月には中東
のエネルギー関連施設や船舶への攻撃も再び強まった。 
○ 供給制約により原油価格は高水準で推移し、運賃や保険料の上昇も調達コストを押し上げて
いる。一方、高値の長期化は世界経済を下押しし、需要減退を招く懸念がある。ＩＥＡは 2026
年の世界石油需要を前年比で日量260 万バレル減と見込んでいる。 
○ 今後の焦点は、11 月 29 日に予定される OPEC プラス閣僚会合での 2027 年の生産枠の協議と
なる。日量約 200 万バレルの協調減産の扱いや、新たな生産基準の設定が論点だが、イラン
情勢が評価作業を複雑にしている。ＩＥＡは 2027 年に供給が日量 770 万バレル増える一方、
需要の回復は同 200 万バレルにとどまると予想しており、供給回復後には需給が緩む可能性
がある。ただし、OPEC とＩＥＡの需要見通しには隔たりがあり、先行きは不透明である。 
○ 日本など輸入国にとっても、原油高の長期化は貿易収支の悪化やインフレ圧力の増幅につな
がる。当面は、OPEC プラスの会合内容、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況、湾岸産油国の生産・輸出
動向、世界の石油需要を注視する必要がある。

【OPECプラス有志７ヵ国は 12 月の生産目標を据え置き、３ヵ月連続で増産を見送る決定】 
OPEC（石油輸出国機構）にロシアなどの主要産油国を加えた OPEC プラスの有志７ヵ国（サウジ
アラビア、ロシア、イラク、クウェート、カザフスタン、アルジェリア、オマーン）は、11 月１日
にオンライン形式で会合を開催し、12 月の原油生産目標を 11 月から据え置くことを決定した。こ
れにより、３ヵ月連続で増産が見送られることとなった。７ヵ国合計の生産目標は日量約3,101 万

### Page 2

2/4 
バレルとなり、９月以来の水準を維持する。今回の声明では、世界の原油市場の動向や先行きを検
討したうえで生産目標の据え置きを決定したと説明するとともに、各国が協調減産の枠組みを順守
する姿勢をあらためて確認した。

【イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴う供給制約を背景に、生産目標と実際の生産量の乖離が続いている】 
OPEC プラス有志国は、2023 年４月に発表し、翌５月から実施した日量 165 万バレルの自主減産に
ついて、段階的な縮小を進めてきた。2026 年９月には、この自主減産の縮小を完了したものの、そ
の後は 10 月、11 月と生産目標を据え置いており、今回も同様の判断を下した。背景には、イラン
情勢の緊迫化を受けて中東地域からの
原油輸送に支障が生じており、各国が生
産目標を引き上げても、実際の供給拡大
につながりにくい状況が続いているこ
とがある（図１）。特に、世界有数の原油
輸送の要衝であるホルムズ海峡では、船
舶の航行を巡る安全上の懸念が残り、原
油輸送に制約が生じている。湾岸産油国
は代替輸送ルートの活用などによって
輸出の回復を図っているものの、多くの
国の原油生産量は依然として生産目標
を下回っている。こうした状況では、生産目標を引き上げても実際の供給拡大につながる効果は限
定的であり、有志国は現行の生産目標を維持することが妥当と判断したと考えられる。 
実際、ＩＥＡ（国際エネルギー機関）が公表した最新の石油市場月報によれば、９月の湾岸産油
国の原油輸出量は日量1,390 万バレルと、前月から同 440 万バレル増加したものの、イラン情勢が
緊迫化する以前の水準の４分の３程度にとどまる。さらに、原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品や
ＬＰＧ（液化石油ガス）の輸出量は依然として戦前の半分以下にとどまり、供給制約の解消には至
っていない。10 月には中東地域のエネルギー関連施設や船舶を対象とする攻撃が再び強まり、輸送
を巡る不確実性も高まっている。このため、OPEC プラスが生産目標を引き上げても、それが実際の
原油供給量の増加に結び付くとは限らず、足元の供給不足を解消するうえでは、イラン情勢の安定
化と原油輸送の正常化が不可欠といえる。

図 1 有志７ヵ国の生産目標と生産量の推移

### Page 3

3/4 
【原油市場では供給制約が価格を下支えする一方、高値の長期化による需要減退も懸念】 
こうした供給制約は、国際原油市況を
押し上げている（図２）。足元の原油価格
は、イラン情勢が緊迫化する以前の水準
を大きく上回るなど、原油市場では供給
不足への警戒が根強く残っている。さら
に、原油価格の高止まりに加えて海上輸
送に伴う運賃や保険料なども上昇してお
り、原油や石油製品の調達コストを押し
上げる要因となっている。こうした状況
の下では、仮に OPEC プラスが増産に踏み
切ったとしても、供給制約が残る限り原
油価格の大幅な下落にはつながりにくいと考えられる。 
一方、原油価格の高止まりが世界経済の下押し圧力となることも懸念される。原油高に伴うエネ
ルギー価格の上昇は、家計の実質購買力を低下させるとともに、企業の生産・輸送コストを押し上
げることで、個人消費や設備投資などの重しとなる。IEA は、最新の石油市場月報において、2026
年の世界の石油需要が前年比で日量 260 万バレル減少するとの見通しを示しており、供給制約に伴
う石油製品の供給不足や価格高騰などが需要を下押しすると見込んでいる。このように需要の減退
が進めば、将来的には原油需給が緩和する可能性もある。そのため、OPEC プラスにとっては、足元
の供給制約への対応だけでなく、原油高が世界経済や石油需要に及ぼす影響にも配慮した政策運営
が求められる。

【今後の焦点は2027 年の生産枠設定、供給回復に伴う需給緩和の可能性も課題】 
先行きについては、OPEC プラスが11 月29 日に予定する次回会合での協議が注目される。今回の
有志７ヵ国による会合とは異なり、次回は OPEC プラス全体の閣僚会合が予定されており、2027 年
の生産枠を巡る協議が主要な焦点になるとみられる。OPEC プラスは、2023 年４月の自主減産につ
いては９月に縮小を完了したものの、別途実施している日量約 200 万バレルの協調減産措置は 2026
年末まで維持している。2027 年以降の政策運営を巡っては、この減産措置の取り扱いが重要な論点
となる。さらに、加盟各国の持続可能な最大生産能力に関する評価を踏まえ、新たな生産基準を設
定する方針であるものの、イラン情勢の悪化に伴う生産設備や生産能力の拡張計画への影響などが
評価作業を複雑化させている。 
その意味では、今後の OPEC プラスの政策運営は一段と難しさを増すと予想される。イラン情勢
の緊迫化が続き、原油輸送の制約が長期化すれば、世界的な供給不足が続くことで原油価格には上
昇圧力が掛かりやすい。一方で、停戦協議の進展などをきっかけに原油輸送が正常化すれば、これ
まで供給が制約されてきた原油が市場に流入することにより、需給環境が一転して緩和する可能性
がある。ＩＥＡは2027 年の世界の石油供給量が前年比で日量770 万バレル増加すると見込む一方、

### Page 4

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
4/4
需要の回復幅は同 200 万バレルにとどまると予想しており、供給回復の進展に伴う需給緩和の可能
性を示唆している。もっとも、OPEC（日量１億 830 万バレル）とＩＥＡ（同１億 440 万バレル）の
需要見通しの間には大きな隔たりがあり、需給の先行きには不確実性が残る。したがって、OPEC プ
ラスにとっては、供給不足が続く現状と、将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらみながら、加盟国間の利
害を調整していく必要がある。
日本をはじめとする原油輸入国にとっても、原油需給を巡る不確実性は景気や物価の先行きを左
右する重要な要因となる。原油価格の高止まりは、輸入額の増加を通じて貿易収支に下押し圧力を
もたらすほか、エネルギー価格や物流コストの上昇を通じてインフレ圧力を増幅させる懸念がある。

一方、原油輸送の正常化によって供給量が回復すれば、原油価格の下落を通じてインフレ圧力が和
らぐことも期待される。ただし、当面は中東情勢を巡る不透明感が根強く、原油価格は地政学的な
動向に左右されやすい状況が続くと考えられる。今後は、次回の OPEC プラス閣僚会合での協議内
容に加え、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況、湾岸産油国の実際の生産・輸出動向、世界の石油需要の変化
などを注意深く見極める必要がある。

Document 380: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-15)

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

WHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM
THE MEASURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO
TURN THE SCREW?
Marianne Mueller and Pascal Devaux
issue 19
21 October 2026
The 2022 energy crisis clearly highlighted the issue of electricity prices in Europe — and, more broadly, the issue of energy prices. As
a result, European manufacturers’ competitiveness has been severely undermined when compared with the United States and China.

By reigniting tensions over electricity prices, the conflict that began in the Middle East in late February 2026 further underscores the
continent’s vulnerability to fossil fuel imports, despite the expansion of renewable energy. Faced with an environment of potential sharp
and sudden price rises, European governments do not have the same room for manoeuvre.
While the additional costs borne by European manufacturers are mainly linked to the share of gas in national electricity mixes, we will

first illustrate that, in Germany, grid operation and maintenance costs, as well as taxation, are also contributing significantly to these
costs.
We will then explore the solutions proposed by the German government under the new European measures regulating state aid to
electricity-intensive industries.
Finally, our estimates will show that, while these measures make Germany more competitive against some European countries, they
do not place it on an equal competitive footing with the United States or China. Nevertheless, Germany has made significant progress

in decarbonising and making its electricity mix more flexible from a European perspective. This should help to ensure that electricity costs
are reduced in the medium term.

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 19 / 21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldWHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM THE MEA- SURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO TURN THE SCREW?
GAS: A KEY FACTOR IN THE ADDITIONAL ELECTRICITY COSTS
FACED BY EUROPEAN INDUSTRY
European manufacturers are still paying significantly higher elec -
tricity costs than their American and Chinese competitors. In 2019,

there was a 30% difference between the prices charged to European
electricity-intensive industries (corresponding to the lower end of the
industrial price range) and the average US industrial price. In 2025,
it stood at nearly 100% according to the IEA (2026) 1, after peaking at
an average of 148% between 2022 and 2023. There is currently a 50%
difference with their Chinese competitors2.
The disruption to Russian gas supplies since 2022 is behind the rising

gas prices, particularly as the EU has had to turn to liquefied natural
gas3 (LNG), which is more expensive than gas transported via pipe -
line and, above all, exposes Europe to global gas market conditions.
The growth of LNG has, in fact, intensified competition between the
major consumption regions, primarily Europe and Asia. European and
Asian prices now tend to move more in tandem (Chart 1). However, in
China, the prevalence of coal — a widely available local resource —

and a more regulated electricity price help to keep industrial prices
lower. At the same time, the United States enjoys plentiful domestic
gas, which is produced at a low cost, driven by the sharp rise in shale
oil and gas production over the past decade. By contrast, since 2022,
Europe has been more exposed to the cost of imported gas (90% of its
total consumption, according to Eurostat), while the United States and
China have enjoyed plentiful domestic resources.

Fluctuations and the level of gas prices are passed on to Eu -
ropean electricity prices via the ‘merit order’ marginal pricing
mechanism, with the wholesale price determined by the margi -
nal cost of the last (and most expensive) electricity source called
upon to meet demand.
As a result, gas plays a decisive role, with gas-fired power sta -
tions setting the price in around 40% of cases in Europe4, with si-
gnificant variations depending on national electricity mixes. It is

close to this average in Germany, below it in Spain (26%) and
France (17%), and above it in Italy (73%) 5 in 2025 6 7. Therefore,
electricity prices are still sensitive to fluctuations in gas prices,
even when electricity is not generated from gas (Chart 2). This
pass-through is particularly pronounced when demand rises
and requires the use of more expensive sources, or when supply
constraints arise (for example, during extreme heatwaves that
reduce wind-power generation or disrupt the cooling of nuclear

power plants).
1 Prices – Electricity 2026 – Analysis – IEA
2 The difference may be even larger for other manufacturers who do not receive the same support as electricity-intensive sectors.
3 Imports of Russian gas (via pipeline and LNG) accounted for around 45% of total European imports in 2021, compared with 14% in Q2 2026 (Bruegel, natural gas imports).
4 ACER, 2026, Key developments in EU electricity and gas markets, 2026 Monitoring report.
5 In Italy, in response to sharply rising gas prices in 2026, the government is currently negotiating a potential option of capping the prices of gas supplied to power stations in

order to limit the rise in the wholesale price of electricity. However, this can only be a temporary general mechanism, with the potentially major cost being borne by public funds
and/or households.
6 Eurelectric, 2026, Power Barometer.
7 EMBER, 2026, Latest energy shock reminds Europe of its risky reliance on gas.
8 Energy shock: 2026 vs. 2022 dashboard – 2nd October 2026 – Economic Studies – BNP Paribas
However, for the time being, the 2026 energy crisis is having a far

smaller effect on electricity prices than in 2022. On the one hand, the
2026 peak reached by the European TTF benchmark is still well below
that recorded in 20228.
In addition, there has been significant progress in decarbonising Eu -
ropean electricity mixes since 2022, which has helped to mitigate, on
average, the pass-through of gas prices to electricity prices.

SOME EUROPEAN COUNTRIES HAVE MANAGED TO STAY
COMPETITIVE, BUT GERMANY IS STILL THE EXCEPTION
Electricity prices partly vary due to countries’ electricity mixes
(Chart 3). Electricity-intensive manufacturers in countries where the
share of fossil energy is high face higher costs (Poland, Italy and Ire -
land) (Chart 4). Conversely, countries with a less carbon-intensive mix

(particularly France and Spain in recent times) are less directly ex -
posed to these fluctuations, with costs closer to the United States and
China (Chart 5).
The situation in Germany is paradoxical, as, despite progressively
greening its electricity mix and wholesale electricity prices (averaging
90 EUR/MWh over the 2023–2025 period) and average electricity costs
(across all industrial sectors) in line with the European average, the most
energy-intensive industries face higher costs than the European average

(and even more so compared with international competitors). This is
mainly due to grid costs and a tax burden above EU standards (Chart 6).
9 Eurostat data are used to compare prices with the rest of the EU. However, given that electricity prices are not uniform for businesses at a European level, there may be differences
between Eurostat and national sources. Jäger P., 2025, “Cautionary tale or model for Europe? How member states should react to Germany’s new electricity subsidy”, Hertie School,

Jacques Delors Centre.
10 P. Devaux, 2026, “European Union: low carbon transition and energy sovereignty, a path fraught with obstacles”
In the first half of 2026, for the most electricity-intensive industries,
the difference compared with the European average was 9%, based on
the prices excluding taxes and levies provided by Eurostat. This gap is
mainly due to the cost of accessing the grid. If all taxes and levies are
included, the difference rises to 21% (Chart 7)9.

EUROPE’S APPROACH OF ADOPTING BOTH STRUCTURAL
PROGRAMMES AND TARGETED SUPPORT MEASURES
European programmes to support the low-carbon transition pursue
objectives relating to climate action (reducing greenhouse gas emis -
sions), sovereignty10 (increasing energy self-sufficiency) and competi -
tiveness (reducing the volatility and costs associated with fossil fuels).
The RePowerEU programme, which was launched in 2022 in response
to the energy crisis, primarily aims to reduce imports of Russian gas

and, more generally, to accelerate the low-carbon transition. Although
Europe has reduced its dependence on imports of Russian fossil fuels,
there has still not been enough progress around the energy transition
and cross-border connectivity. This is due to governance constraints

and struggles in implementing the programme in practice within each
Member State11.
State aid is still the main vehicle for this. In response to the 2021–
2022 energy crisis, the European Union temporarily relaxed the rules
on this type of aid in order to support businesses facing rising energy
prices. Any intervention of this kind, provided that it is selective and

could affect trade within the single market, must be authorised in ad -
vance by the European Commission in principle. Since June 2025, the
Clean Industrial Deal State Aid Framework (CISAF) has provided a more
sustainable framework for these kinds of interventions. In particular,
this framework, which runs until the end of 2030, authorises Member
States to grant temporary aid to electricity-intensive businesses to off-
set the cost of electricity. The aid must be targeted and come with

decarbonisation commitments12. Against the backdrop of the crisis in
the Middle East, the temporary METSAF scheme, adopted in April 2026,
helped (in relation to measures falling under the CISAF) to increase the
proportion of additional costs offset by governments from 50% to 70%,
thereby offering additional support to the most at-risk sectors.
GERMANY IS STILL SUFFERING FROM INSUFFICIENT IN-
VESTMENT IN ITS ELECTRICITY NETWORKS, WHICH THE
AID SCHEMES ARE ATTEMPTING TO OFFSET

The issue of electricity costs is a particularly hot issue in Germany,
given the importance of industry to the economy (around 20% of va -
lue added, one of the highest levels in Europe) and its exposure to
international competition. Furthermore, the delay in modernising
electricity infrastructure (only 22.6% of the high-voltage lines planned
for 2025 had actually been completed by that date) is causing bottle -
necks between the production areas in the north and the consump -

11 European Court of Auditors, 2026, “Implementation of the REPowerEU plan must be stepped up”.
12 It applies to the most electricity-intensive activities, which are seeing their competitiveness threatened by international competition, and follows the 4x50 principle: 50% of
electricity consumption is eligible for a subsidy; Of the eligible proportion, the subsidy may not exceed 50% of the wholesale price;
The final wholesale price paid by the company must not be less than €50/MWh;

50% of the aid received must be reinvested in decarbonisation (renewable energy generation, storage and improvements in energy efficiency); Around 90 sectors are covered by this
reform, accounting for approximately 2,000 companies, including in the chemical, steel and paper industries. The scheme, which is strictly regulated, will run for just three years
(from 1st January 2026 to the end of 2028) at an estimated total cost of between EUR 3 and 5 bn, financed by the special Climate and Transformation Fund.

13 Bundesnetzagentur Monitoring Report (2015) and Bundesnetzagentur Monitoring Report (2025). These costs mainly cover the costs of physical infrastructure; operational,
regulatory and compliance costs; and costs associated with using the transmission and distribution network.
14 The Industriestrompreis (industrial electricity price) is the public subsidy mechanism aiming to reduce the price of electricity for some industries.
15 The indirect carbon cost compensation mechanism is a government support measure for electricity-intensive industries, which aims to offset the cost of carbon-emission

allowances for energy producers.
tion centres in the south. As a result, the costs of accessing services
have increased fivefold over 10 years (rising from EUR 1 bn in 2014
to EUR 5.2 bn in 2024 13). At the same time, as we have already seen,
taxation and grid charges are hitting industry hard, with levies on large
consumers far exceeding the European average despite some support
mechanisms being put in place for the most electricity-intensive com-

panies (such as the Bandlastprivileg measure to reduce grid tariffs).
As a result, German industrial production has suffered in particular
due to rising energy prices in recent years, including electricity, with
electricity-intensive industries being especially affected (Chart 8).
In order to cope with these higher electricity costs than the European
average, Germany has been using European support schemes to reduce
the cost of electricity for businesses, particularly since the Merz go -

vernment took office in May 2025. The main scheme — the Industries-
trompreis14, made possible by the CISAF — has been approved by the
European Commission for the 2026–2028 period. While it is less bene-
ficial than the indirect carbon cost (ICC) offsetting included in electri -
city prices15, it has a broader scope, meaning that businesses ineligible
for the ICC can benefit from it. Other measures complement this targe-
ted support by addressing other components of the electricity bill. As a

result, the electricity tax has been set at the European minimum for
manufacturing sectors, while the gas-storage tax has been abolished.
The government is also subsidising grid fees, which should halve them.
Overall, according to our estimates based on 2024 prices, the final
price of electricity for these industries could fall by around a quarter
(to 116 EUR/MWh for industries with annual consumption of between
70 and 150 GWh, compared with a European average of 123 EUR/MWh)

(Chart 9). Germany’s advantage over Central and Eastern European
countries, where electricity costs are generally already higher, looks
set to increase.

However, even when taking all of the subsidies into account, the price
